* [ ] **Q6 · Compare memory topologies on one workload** · 17 h, range 10–24 · 1.2% · X · after the M8a gate
  * **Compare three candidates on the workload each actually satisfies**: a smaller qualified all-SRAM configuration, the two-class configuration R-15-247 states, and SRAM beside an explicit public-weight store.
  * **For the public-weight candidate, stream bounded chunks authenticated against a trusted model identity**, keep authority-bearing state and the verification buffers local, import neither executable authority nor external capability tags, and specify buffering, the integrity-failure behaviour and the physical requester interface.
  * **Account for link bandwidth, verification throughput, scratch buffers, loading time, storage energy and visible access patterns.** Public weights do not make prompt-dependent sparse accesses public, and an external device can deny service, so no hard delivery is promised without a separately justified bound.
  * **Use supplied or measured physical coefficients**, ECC, tags where needed, periphery, routing, refresh, repair and thermal corners included, and ask a foundry or macro provider for realizable supply rather than literature cell density. R-15-247m wants the measured macro and R-17-063b books the stack as an offering of no foundry; R4 states the second class's architecture ahead of this comparison, as the definition of the candidate it weighs, and R5 is where the qualification evidence lands, which this comparison precedes.
  * **Decide the topology and the address-growth story while the format is undeployed.** R-15-007d freezes the width at the profile and stored authority is what makes the freeze permanent, so the decision is taken before a deployed population turns it into a migration; explicit block storage need not be mapped into the capability address space, and wider load and store addressing is a separate decision, a register act this item raises and does not take.
  * **Done when** the candidates are compared on useful workload, total resources, implementation maturity and physical threat claims, and the selected product has a credible supply path; a capacity-only winner that cannot deliver the required bandwidth loses. Ramulator2 serves a conventional-DRAM alternative and OpenRAM an SRAM macro exploration under a suitable technology setup, both conditionally: neither supplies or qualifies the proposed oxide-semiconductor stack, Ramulator's default out-of-order front end, cache and dynamic scheduling are not a model of this machine, so the trace and the controller policy are supplied, and an OpenRAM model in another process is not a scaled prediction of this one. The strongest route to much more physical storage changes the one-die realization (R-15-162) and its absence of an external memory bus, can expand accessible model data without widening directly addressable RAM, and may make a larger model possible while making each token slower.
* [ ] **Q7 · Decide bulk remanence deliberately** · 10 h, range 6–14 · 0.7% · X · after the M8a gate
  * **Decide whether abrupt-power-loss confidentiality for sensitive bulk is required for the intended deployment.** R-17-058f books the second class's remanence as a residual no construction closes, and it does not disappear behind capability-tag invalidation, which retires stale authority and not plaintext. The threat decision can be taken ahead of the rest of this item.
  * **If it is required, compare three arms**: keeping sensitive data in protected local storage, reducing the retained sensitive bulk, and an encrypted bulk path whose ephemeral key has an independently justified lifetime and destruction mechanism, R-17-059a scoping the attacker who reaches the plaintext after the key is gone.
  * **For an adversary-controlled memory interface, include authentication, freshness, address and generation binding, and atomic data-and-metadata updates**, from a defined standard construction and never a new cipher or mode.
  * **Measure** masked cryptographic throughput, metadata storage, boot and power-loss behaviour and transition latency, and keep ciphertext confidentiality apart from the access-pattern, power, live-probe and denial-of-service risks that remain.
  * **Done when** the design either has a concrete protection construction with evidence for its threat window, or the product declines the deployments needing that property. The first arm changes R-15-199, the memory path carrying no cryptography, and is a register act; the second is scope control and not hardening. The existing cryptographic proof and oracle work comes first, and a verified primitive or a masked AES block is not a verified memory-protection subsystem. The gain is a defined physical threat window closed at more area, metadata, effort and latency, with any performance benefit indirect, such as a larger conventional medium becoming acceptable.
* [ ] **Q8 · Accelerate only a measured bottleneck** · 17 h, range 10–24 · 1.2% · X · after the M8a gate
  * **Profile the target path before any new core mechanism**, separating front-end issue, pointer dependencies, bank stalls, memory traffic, vector utilization and masked-crypto throughput, over the composed image M7.1 boots.
  * **Try the static improvements first**: layout, loop transformations, bounded tiling, supported vector lowering and the fusion the profile already admits, with code size and working set measured beside cycles.
  * **For a media or arithmetic hotspot that remains, compare a bounded verified hardware operation with the software**, with state, data movement, reset, authority, constant-time obligations where relevant and proof maintenance all in the comparison.
  * **Reuse the Ara, Gemmini and OpenTitan material [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) already names** where it fits, assuming nothing about an imported block's bus, DMA, timing or verification contract carrying over unchanged.
  * **Done when** the composed workload improves at acceptable quality and energy without breaking its timing and protection contracts, each architectural alternative in an estimate scenario of its own and the current-design baseline unmodified. The gain is potentially large on the accelerated fraction and not a scalar uplift; many optimizations and accelerator families are already assumed in the estimates, so implementing one realizes a budgeted benefit rather than earning it again, and a codec accelerator is not a shortcut merely because fixed-function hardware is admissible.
* [ ] **Q9 · Restore compatibility and local autonomy selectively** · 14 h, range 8–20 · 1.0% · X · after the M8a gate
  * **Pick an actual blocked application or workflow before defining compatibility**, and try a small explicit-authority API subset rather than a POSIX personality.
  * **For development autonomy, support bounded source processing and artifacts as data first**, preserving the native-code admission boundary: R-02-008 keeps the machine from building its own software and R-13-001c keeps the composer off the device, and a compiler library supplies neither the target's certificates nor a local composer.
  * **Consider a confined external-authenticator client** with bounded protocol handling and no arbitrary DMA, against R-12-020's exclusion, stating that the key's credential behaviour stays an external trust dependency even where it cannot compromise platform isolation.
  * **Measure the external generation service's build, proof, transfer, restart and recovery times**, R-13-001a making every install a generation rather than an amendment; identical artifacts and proof results are cached only by complete dependency identity and invalidated when semantics, configuration or assumptions change, and no cache bypasses a required check.
  * **Done when** a named workflow works within an explicit capability, resource and trust contract, with the policy changes it needs approved, and no speculative compatibility layer is built before a consumer exists. The gain is usability and code reuse more than processor performance; local development, protocol support and autonomy add work first, reuse may repay it, and the authenticator decision strengthens no threat model by itself.
* [ ] **Q10 · Choose the product the slice demonstrates** · 18 h, range 12–24 · 1.3% · I · after the M8a gate
  * **Build the minimal production-path vertical slice**: bounded input, a capability transfer, a persistent operation, a controlled failure and a restart, on the same device binary across the golden emulator and the RTL in co-simulation, which is the identity M8a and M8b already hold.
  * **Exercise adverse but ordinary conditions**: simultaneous work, full pools, an interrupted update, loss of the composition service, thermal limits, and recovery inside the declared fault model.
  * **Revise scope explicitly if the simultaneous mobile floor does not close on credible resources.** A smaller all-SRAM appliance or a host-executed model can each be a useful deliverable, and neither satisfies an unchanged mobile hardware claim under R-18-004a; which is wanted is the product's owner's call, and this item states the arms and their forfeits rather than taking one.
  * **Done when** useful behaviour, implementation correspondence, resource accounting and physical feasibility meet at one configuration. A host product is a different assurance scope, its host and runtime becoming dependencies, and not an optimized-binary shortcut to the original theorem.

* [ ] **Q11 · Bound the reach of a run-ahead fetch, or record that it is unbounded** · 6 h, range 4–8 · 0.4% · I
  * **What the register constrains is the dependence, and what nothing constrains is the extent.** R-15-022 confines run-ahead to the statically determined path; R-15-104 admits a fetch-path state element whose contents are a function of the fetched stream and refuses one whose write data depends on a prior execution; R-15-165 and R-15-217 restate that same test, over the retained fast structures and over the fourth flush class. Which addresses the static-path fetch buffer may request is therefore decided, and decided normatively. What no artifact states, [the register](requirements-register.md) and [the spec](spec.md) alike, is a bound on the **range** or the **depth** of that reach: nothing forbids the successor chain running sequentially past the end of a code region, no entry requires a run-ahead request to be bounds-checked against the executing PCC before it issues, R-15-104 declines size and run-ahead depth as criteria by name, and R-15-108's enumerated frozen-parameter set carries no fetch-buffer depth.
  * **The bound belongs in the register, and the absence contract cannot hold it.** [The absence contract](absence-contract.md) adds no obligation of its own and is a derived view (R-15-100a), and its register is one row per claimed absence of a structure (R-15-100, R-15-100a). A constraint on what an admitted structure may reach claims no absence and names no synthesis setting, so it fits neither that grammar nor the binding grammar [the provenance record](../rtl/synthesis-provenance.md) admits, whose every `n/a` ground is about a structure the build does not contain. The act is an amendment to R-15-104, or a new entry beside it in [the register](requirements-register.md)'s §15.14 taking a letter suffix, with [the contract's §5](absence-contract.md#5-the-decision-rule-table-freeness) and the spec's commentary following the register rather than leading it.
  * **The two bounds are decided separately, having different natures.** A depth bound is a frozen parameter or it is nothing: R-15-108 enumerates the frozen microarchitectural parameter set and no fetch-buffer depth is in it, so one arm adds it there and the other admits the depth and states the ground. A range bound is an issue-time check, whether a run-ahead request must lie inside the executing PCC's bounds or inside the region the fetched instruction came from, and refusing it is a decision this item takes rather than a silence it leaves. **Neither arm is decidable by a run on this tree**: the Sail model carries no fetch buffer and no run-ahead at all, which is the separation R-15-098 states between the two registers, and inexpressibility is what routes the question out of the refinement, not what routes it into the contract's rows.
  * **Two open questions elsewhere read this decision, and neither is closed here.**
    * **The `fence.t` term is priced on its own ground and not on the store side's.** R-15-217 puts the static-path fetch buffer in the stream-determined class, emptied by the fence's pipeline drain, while R-15-218's padded constant is stated over the store buffer's drain alone and R-15-015b keeps device-space stores out of that buffer precisely so that no endpoint's accept latency sits inside the constant. An outstanding run-ahead fetch toward a slow endpoint is therefore a term neither entry prices, which is an argument for the reach bound. The endpoint-reach rule is not what sets that constant: R-15-015c and R-15-015d set the *compartment's* own bound that way, and R-15-218 with R-15-015b keep every endpoint's accept latency outside the fence's.
    * **The boot ROM's non-executable region has one ground, it is contingent, and closing that is R1c-ii's act rather than this item's.** The disposition, that nothing on this die fetches from `platform.boot_rom` because R-09-006 has the root of trust place the verified M-mode image in main SRAM and release the boot core into it, is recorded in R1c-ii's findings block and at F-207 and F-229b, and it rests on that one boot-flow fact: change the flow and the ground goes with it. **R1c-ii has not landed**, its own note declaring no tier because two of the top's three halves are blocked, so no landed act carries this reading and no second ground is owed at any gate that has run. The connection is one-way: if this item takes the bounding arm, a range bound stated at issue against the executing PCC refuses a fetch into that region without depending on the boot flow, and R1c-ii's gate has a second and non-contingent ground to read; if it takes the other arm, that gate reads the single contingent one.
  * **Done when** the reach of a run-ahead fetch request has a stated bound in the register, or the register states that it is bounded in neither range nor depth and carries the ground on which that is admitted, with [the contract's §5](absence-contract.md#5-the-decision-rule-table-freeness) and the spec's commentary agreeing with whichever arm is taken and every co-read pair the edit moves blessed by hand. A bound stated only in the spec closes nothing, the spec being commentary; a row added to [the contract's §3](absence-contract.md#3-the-register) closes nothing either, that register being one row per claimed absence. Rejecting the bound completes the item, as it does everywhere in this section.
  * **Acceptance predicate, stated over the checker because the act is a document act and no run in this tree can see a fetch buffer.** The item closes when the register carries the decision in one entry under §15.14; that entry carries a criterion that decides, so a candidate fetch path passes or fails it by reading the entry alone rather than by a judgment beside it; `tools/check.py` is green with every mutant killed, which is what holds the contract's agreement with the register in both directions (R-15-100a); and every pair the edit moves is blessed through `run.py coread --bless` rather than cleared by `--fix`. One repair is inside the same surface and is taken in the same act rather than left beside it: R-15-022's Accept line cites R-15-152 for the static-path fetch buffer, R-15-152 being the camera's mechanical shutter, where R-15-165 is the entry that names the buffer. **The landing is Tier A**, a register edit being one, so no holder citation is owed and no new checker rule is a precondition; an entry under §15.14 with an existing trace falls in coverage cells that already exist, so no line and no column is added.
* [ ] **Q12 · Re-derive the inference floor against a ternary weight format and state its prompt-processing term** · 10 h, range 6–14 · 0.7% · X
  * **Re-run the static predicates over a natively ternary weight file, restating each predicate rather than only its number.** Three of the four are [the demand report](inference-demand.md)'s section 5: weight bytes, bytes read per generated token, and KV bytes per token in two cache formats. The fourth, the resident buffers the loader allocates at an 8,192-token context, is its section 6, so a re-derivation aimed at section 5 alone reproduces three of the four and misses that the fourth is stated per cache format. Section 3's licence and publisher-conversion discipline is re-run on the candidate at the candidate's own revision, and the file's digest is checked against the publisher's tree listing before it is loaded, exactly as that section does for the weight it chose.
  * **Take the multiple between two measured whole-file densities and never between two nominal widths.** The format R-18-004a(vii) calls four-bit costs more than four bits per parameter once its block scales and its wider embedding and down-projections are counted, which section 5 measures and section 8's projection carries; a shipped ternary file likewise carries a shared scale per block of weights, so its whole-file density is above the nominal figure its own literature quotes. A multiple taken between a nominal four bits and a nominal 1.58 is two errors that cancel, and it is not the move on the term the report says binds.
  * **Carry no from-scratch constraint.** Converting a trained full-precision model to native ternary weights after the fact is a published pipeline (BitNet Distillation, arXiv:2510.13998) rather than an open problem, and the candidate below declares as its base the model the report already measures, so an item recording quantization-aware training from scratch as an honest constraint would put a false claim into the register on the day it landed.
  * **The candidate, and what disqualifies the obvious one.** `prism-ml/Ternary-Bonsai-4B-gguf` is a natively ternary model at or above the size the member states, published with its own GGUF conversion and declaring `Qwen/Qwen3-4B` as its base, which is the base section 3 selected, so section 5's table and section 7's comparator re-run against a directly comparable file rather than against a different model; its terms are read at its own revision under section 3's discipline before it is loaded, and that reading is what admits or refuses it. Microsoft's BitNet b1.58, the one ternary weight format [the alternatives survey](architectural-alternatives.md) cites, passes that discipline on both limbs and fails on size alone, below the parameter count the member states. That survey cites it as the concession half of an entry whose disposition already records the weight-format lever as adopted, so what this item adds is not the mechanism but the arithmetic no artifact has taken.
  * **Split the measurement by what the pinned instrument reaches.** The static predicates are read from the file's own header by a tool section 2's pin already builds, so the bytes half needs no new instrument and is the load-bearing half. A timed row does: the candidate's block type is carried by a vendor fork and not by the pin, which is a second instrument pin, a second [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) row with its terms read at that pin, and the first file under [inference-demand/](inference-demand/) whose build pair differs from every other one there. The timed half waits on that pin being judged worth taking, and the item may decline it and say so.
  * **Score the result against R-18-004b's second-class capacity and bandwidth comparisons, beside the rows already there, and name the register act that follows.** R-18-004a(vii) states the member's format with the same grammatical force as it states its parameter count, so a ternary member is an amendment to that entry measured against the same recorded floor (R-18-004d) and never a re-scoring against it, and R-18-004d's versioned demonstration set names the member it amends. The alternatives survey's ternary entry records the weight-format lever as adopted at no specification surface, and an amendment to R-18-004a(vii) is specification surface, so that disposition moves in the same act or the two disagree. What the projection over the published file sizes says the measurement will find is a weight stream inside the grant floor R-18-004b states, which no configuration in the report reaches; that is a demand fitting the stated minimum and not a ground for moving it, R-18-004b's figures being floors on supply and not caps on demand, as section 8 already says of them.
  * **State a prompt-processing term where the register states only a sustained generation rate.** R-18-004a(vii) floors a generating rate over a declared context, and R-12-085's declared ceiling names the resident bytes, the context length, the quantization formats, the expert count and top-k, the KV footprint per token and the bank grant, so a composition meeting every one of them and answering its first token arbitrarily late passes both. The distinction itself is not missing: [the estimates](performance-estimates.md) already carry prompt processing and token generation as two claim rows with two comparators, and the report measures the two separately while declining to carry any host rate into the target. What is missing is a quantity in a normative entry, and R-18-004a's own criterion makes a member carrying no quantity a candidate can miss a review-gate finding, so the term is stated as a quantity or it is not stated at all. *Decode* is not the word for it here, R-18-004b's aggregate reserving that word for the video frame pool; the two phases are prompt processing and token generation, which the outside literature calls prefill and decode.
  * **The grant stays single, and only the admitted rate can be stated per phase.** R-15-247p whole-binds banks to islands and lets no bank count vary with occupancy or load, and R-15-188 has the shared resources, the NoC, the memory controller and main memory, never scale, so a bank grant differing between the two phases either moves banks across an island boundary, which R-15-247p refuses, or names no quantity at all, the island holding the larger of the two at every instant either way. The register's own shape for two enumerated modes of unequal demand is R-15-236b's and R-15-238c's: admit at the binding point and let the lighter one run inside it as slack. This item therefore takes no per-phase grant, and a proposal for one is an R-15-247p amendment reaching [the bank-count contract](bank-count-dse-contract.md) and the rule that holds it, which is a different item and a different price.
  * **A two-valued operating point is admissible in one shape, and the item names that shape before proposing any.** R-11-017 has the admission proof select each partition's operating point and emit the assignment as one artifact, and R-15-188 makes that point a composition-time constant switched only at partition boundaries, so the two phases carry two operating points only as two admitted partitions on the composition-fixed wheel, each with its own constant. One partition whose point is chosen at a data-dependent transition is the load-following [the absence contract](absence-contract.md)'s A-12 excludes by its evidence column, whose admitted inputs are the composed assignment and the mode index alone; and a global mode change per phase is what R-11-018 and R-15-189 admit only as rare, RoT-attested and on explicit authority, which a transition driven by prompt length is not, R-11-019 being the register's own statement of why an interaction-rate transition is not that axis. The admissible shape costs no new mechanism and is not free: R-15-220 already carries operating-point relock as a switch term and R-11-009 counts the switch-duty ratio explicitly, so the switch constant and the visit rate both move and R-11-011's pinning threshold is where that lands. Whether to take the shape is a decision this item states and does not take, the energy it buys reaching the compute island alone because the memory side never scales.
  * **This is neither Q4a re-opened nor Q4b brought forward.** Q4a is landed and its note is frozen, so what its report concludes is amended by a record beside it rather than by a growth of that item's asks; Q4b is the target-kernel half behind the M8a gate, and nothing here measures a target, this item's budget being derived from bytes, the register's own rate and the register's own supply floors exactly as Q4a's report derives it. What this item adds to Q4a is a weight format its ladder does not contain and a term its verdict does not carry; what it leaves to Q4b is every timed target figure.
  * **The measurement is owed and it is runnable today, and it is taken when this item runs rather than now.** Nothing in the static half waits on an image, a kernel or a bank grant: the candidate is published, its terms are readable at its own revision, and the header read runs on the instrument section 2 already pins. The item is the act that takes it, and no figure from it is quoted anywhere until it has been.
  * **Done when** the ternary configuration's static demand is recorded under [inference-demand/](inference-demand/) on the report's own predicates and scored against R-18-004b's second-class capacity and bandwidth comparisons beside the four-bit rows already there; the register act the result implies is either taken or stated with what it costs and what else moves with it; and R-18-004a(vii) and R-12-085 either carry a prompt-processing quantity a candidate can miss or the item records why none is writable and names what owes it. Quality is not decided here, the comparator being section 7's and the threshold Q1's, and a narrower weight buys nothing until that threshold admits it. A projection is not a measurement, a host figure is not a target figure, and neither half of this item lowers a floor.
  * **Acceptance predicate at entry, in two halves, and only the first is writable today.** The first half closes when a static record for the ternary file sits under [inference-demand/](inference-demand/) produced by the same predicate the four-bit rows were produced by, its digest checked against the publisher's tree listing at a named revision before the file was loaded, and its budget columns computed by the same arithmetic as the rows beside it; a row whose predicate differs from the rows it sits beside fails this rather than extending the table. The second half's predicate is that R-18-004a(vii) or R-12-085 names a prompt-processing quantity in the unit of the clause it feeds, with R-18-004d's set stating the member's pass condition in that unit. **That predicate may not be writable at this item**, and the refusal is recorded here on M5.3's precedent where it is not: no artifact measures a target prompt-processing rate, the report's own rule forbids carrying a host one into the target, and the earliest artifact that could supply one is Q4b's behind the M8a gate. A quantity taken from the host to satisfy the predicate fails it.
* [ ] **Q13 · Pin the bring-up Wasm interpreter and re-read its ranking against the absent predictors** · 11.5 h, range 7–16 · 0.8% · X
  * **Pin a release where the paragraph names only a lineage.** [The porting guide's Servo entry](userspace-porting.md#servo-the-contained-browser-engine) names a wasmi-lineage pure-Rust interpreter standing in as the unverified bring-up interim and names no release, and no register entry names that interim at all, so this is the first version pin that paragraph takes rather than a bump. R-14-013a is the engine the interim stands in front of and not the interim itself, and the pin is recorded where the interim is defined. Say in the same act which of the adopted upstream changes are expected to survive curation into that engine and which are interim-only, because each of them is surface the R-14-013a refinement against R-14-013b's pinned semantics eventually covers.
  * **Two of the adopted changes are already in tension with the register and are booked before they are taken.** Function bodies held in append-only buckets that never move for the engine's lifetime buy baked-in body pointers by never releasing, and R-14-015 wants a declared selection and release algorithm inside a composition-sized arena under the R-08-046 bounded-pool contract; adopting the design into the interim and discovering at curation that it cannot be carried is the expensive version of this item. The upstream's artifact-size knob that disables guest validation carries the precondition that the embedder controls its inputs, which is the opposite of the case R-14-013a exists for, untrusted dynamic content under robust guest confinement, so it is admitted only where the module is image-resident and admitted and never on the R-14-008a delivery path. Neither is adopted outright.
  * **The branch-site inversion is a measurement-correctness matter and not a speed one.** Upstream's single largest gain comes from un-collapsing a `csel` back into two branch sites, undoing what `DestinationPropagation`, a Rust 1.92 MIR pass, had merged: CoreMark rises from about 2800 to over 4200 on an Apple M2 Pro, which [the engine's 2.0 release post](https://wasmi-labs.github.io/blog/posts/wasmi-v2.0/) states as its most important single change. The mechanism is a dynamic predictor getting two entries with separable histories instead of one entry with mixed history. Rows A-04, A-05 and A-06 of [the absence contract](absence-contract.md) remove the direction predictor, the target predictor and the return-address stack, prediction being static-only with zero mutable predictor state, so the structure the gain rests on does not exist here and the upstream fix is not a fix on this die. Letting the MIR pass collapse the two paths is the cheaper act, and it is a build the item takes rather than a revert it authors.
  * **The porting note that generalizes it, and it extends a sentence the register already carries.** Any interpreter result that improves by feeding a branch predictor is measuring hardware this machine does not have, so the whole upstream benchmark ranking is re-read entry by entry under A-04, A-05 and A-06 before any of it informs a figure, and an entry whose gain rests on predictor, target-predictor or return-stack behaviour is recorded as unread on this target rather than carried across. R-14-015's Accept already states the narrow case, a dispatch loop on a machine with static branch prediction and no instruction cache being the worst tenant a bulk fetch constant could have, so the note is that sentence generalized from the arena to the ranking. The same reading is owed of the two changes that trade dependent chains for offsets, the flattened instance layout and the baked-in body pointers, whose value on a machine with no data cache and no prefetcher (A-07, A-09, A-10) is expected to be larger here than upstream measures and for which upstream reports no isolated figure at all.
  * **Narrow R-14-008a rather than reopen it: what is undecided is direct versus indirect threading.** That entry already admits threaded dispatch by name as one of its ahead-of-time levers, so the open question is only the form. Upstream reports indirect-threaded roughly 10 to 15 percent slower than direct-threaded and significantly smaller in the memory its IR occupies, which is a trade against R-14-008b's footprint budget rather than against a policy. Direct threading makes the IR a buffer of code pointers, and that stays inside the deleted class for a structural reason rather than a judgment: the buffer is data, so it consumes no executable SRAM budget, which is the capacity measurement the static-code-overlay trigger is stated as, and no component fills an executable bank at run time, which is the runtime write-then-execute consumer R-15-047's exclusion rests on. **A threading scheme that inlines handler bodies rather than pointers is a different mechanism and is already refused**, R-14-008b's Accept admitting no set on the ground that it is data rather than generated code. Direct threading's own unpriced cost sits in the typed-assembly derivation and it is narrower than it first looks: R-05-112 already contemplates sentries loaded from vtables and R-05-113 already requires an indirect transfer's code type to carry the finite set of labels its reachable sentries target, so the mechanism and its typing obligation both exist; what has no rule is a `code`-typed field at a slot inside a runtime-written buffer, [the typed assembly language](typed-assembly-language.md) typing a register file at block entry and carrying no general memory typing former. Defaulting to indirect defers that addition whole, which is the argument for taking it as the default until the typing is priced.
  * **Two register acts this item raises and does not take.** **R-14-013b declares the guest semantics version-frozen and names no version**, and no Wasm version number appears anywhere in the tracked corpus, so the freeze has no frontier written down; the act is to name the frozen version and record Wasm 3.0 as declined rather than not-yet-reached. The version is the tighter instrument than a proposal list, Wasm 3.0 being a language version whose additions reach past the typed references, exception handling and garbage collection an engine roadmap names, and it is written *Wasm 3.0* so that it is never read as the engine's own release number. Its garbage-collection half needs no new ground: R-14-015 wants fixed object-size classes or a statically proved bounded representation, a maximum live object count per class, a declared selection and release algorithm and, where guest-level collection exists, a complete WCET and non-interference model, none of which Wasm GC as specified supplies, and its Accept names the collector shapes nothing here may rely on. **The deterministic profile is the one named exception the decline carries**, and it narrows exactly one thing, the choice of NaN payload inside R-14-013b's pinned numeric semantics: its relaxed-vector half has nothing to act on, R-14-013d admitting fixed-width SIMD only, and it delivers no blanket determinism, memory and table growth staying nondeterministic by design. It is booked as a pinned-semantics amendment **and** as a build-configuration fact, R-14-013a's Accept requiring both theorems to check against the pinned semantics at build, so an engine built without the feature would not refine an amended pin. R-14-013d's curation rule gates it and the question is asked before the amendment is drafted: the semantics enters the pin from the tracked mechanized upstream rather than by authorship, so whether that lineage reaches the profile decides whether the exception is writable. **Neither act moves the §16 replay record**, whose members are drawn values crossing an interface and not a guest language's choices.
  * **Done when** the porting paragraph names the pinned upstream release and says which adopted changes are expected to survive curation, the threading form is chosen with its reason recorded, the ranking is re-read entry by entry with the predictor-fed entries marked unread on this target, and each register act above is either taken at its entry or declined with a reason. A faster interim is not the payoff and nothing here is a credit against the no-JIT cost R-14-008f books as accepted; what the item buys is a ranking that means something on this machine and an interim whose design does not have to be undone at curation. Adopting an upstream change into the unverified interim is not an admission of it into the R-14-013a engine, and this item takes no step toward the memory typing the direct-threaded form would want.
  * **Acceptance predicate at the documents and the host, and a stated gate at the machine.**
  * **At the documents and the host the predicate is three readings and one build.** (i) Every entry in the upstream benchmark suite carries one of two verdicts, *reads on this target* or *predictor-fed and unread here*, the second naming the structure the gain rests on, and the `csel` entry is of the second kind. (ii) A case-insensitive search of [docs/](.) for the engine's name returns the porting paragraph, this item and its note, and nothing inside [the estimates](performance-estimates.md), which carries no figure from this upstream and no CoreMark figure at all until the gate below is passed. (iii) Two host builds of the dispatch handler, one as the MIR pass leaves it and one with the branch sites separated, differ at their dispatch sites, which is the difference the machine half would measure; a host cycle count over either is recorded as a host figure and carried into no estimate.
  * **At the machine there is no predicate, and the reason is that no item before R3 puts a composed image on the elaborated core.** The configuration the question is asked of is the one whose predictor parameters [the provenance record](../rtl/synthesis-provenance.md) binds to A-04, A-05 and A-06, and the cycle separation between the two dispatch forms is the profiling Q8 opens over the image R3 boots. R-15-101 makes the comparison well posed there, cycle-level timing being a function of the instruction stream and architectural state alone, so the two forms differ by a decidable property of the stream rather than by learned history; what that is worth in cycles is Q8's to report and not this item's to assert. Until it is reported no figure from this upstream enters [the estimates](performance-estimates.md), which is the whole of what the guard above holds.
* [ ] **Q14 · Reach the third matrix track, and record what already answers in-memory compute** · 5 h, range 3–7 · 0.3% · I
  * **The scope check is already written down, so it is cited rather than restated.** [The profile](isa-profile.md)'s §3 table carries a re-pin target per unit: the matrix extension's is the ratified three-track set (R-15-009) and the CHERI dialect's is none, the RVY re-pin being retired rather than deferred (R-15-007, R-17-048a), and the profile's standing-obligation paragraphs draw the same contrast in prose. What this item may add is one cross-reference where the comparison below would otherwise read that retirement as covering the profile. The ground the profile states for the retirement is the field widths placing the format outside anything the standard will ratify; R-15-007d's permanent-format commitment is why this profile cannot later change its own format, which is a different argument, so the two are cited apart.

R-15-123's no-foreign-computers line decides against every commodity part in this class, which runs its stack as firmware on a hidden core, so what is weighed is verified RTL or nothing. **The endpoint is priced where the register prices it, and that is not at `fence.t`.** R-15-015b keeps a device-space store out of the store buffer so that R-15-218's padded per-class constant is not set by the slowest endpoint's accept latency, and it is there that R-12-046's divided card clock is named the on-die existence proof that such an endpoint is reachable at all. A second slow endpoint therefore sets the bound R-15-015c states, device authority provisioned by latency class in the attested devicetree (R-09-007), and R-15-015d is what decides whether it earns a thin driver compartment or spends a divisor for nothing.

What is genuinely new is the compartment that answers as a card, which runs the discipline in the opposite direction to the AKA client's, and R-12-084a with R-15-238d is the containment footing it takes: a compartment holding one session's ring and nothing else, minting nothing, naming no device, and faulting as a contained crash. The arm is taken by stating that compartment or refusing it, not by re-proposing the parser.

The trade is written in the shape R-17-051 already uses for the generation floor, a coverage-for-security cost stated rather than hidden, and R-04-011 is what refuses reading a second tolerated foreign computer out of the first, the eUICC being an exception and not a pass. The two entries that move are R-15-146, whose enable is driven by grant liveness and carries an in-use indication no compromised component can suppress, which a part answering an RF field while the host is off holds nothing to satisfy, and R-15-147, whose cut set is the microphone, the camera and the USB data lanes with the radio staying page-reachable because the paging task holds its capability: a power-reserve element would be the first candidate in that set with no capability holder to justify a carve-out. R-18-004a(i)'s IMS voice call is a demonstration a candidate owes and R-17-051's IMS emergency bearer is a coverage statement, so what the register carries is an obligation to show a call and never a stack a messaging transport could ride. The arm is therefore two compartments and two verified wire grammars on R-12-040's pattern rather than one, and the first thing it must settle is whether the client that call runs over is owed by that member or by this decision.
  * **Price the primitives against what §5 freezes, not against what the protocol assumes.** The group-messaging standard RCS encryption rests on, MLS (RFC 9420), fixes a mandatory ciphersuite over HPKE, HKDF, X25519 and Ed25519 beside a hash, and no requirement names one of the four. R-05-058a freezes the platform's own choices at Category 5 by parameter set and confines its external-profile exception to the wire, "never the device's own long-lived keys", which a messaging identity credential is; R-10-024 freezes AES-GCM as the filesystem cipher, "one cipher and not a menu", so the one primitive the two have in common is already sited in another role. A pre-quantum suite entering here is an act taken against R-05-058a, and a protocol that negotiates its suite across providers meets R-12-043a's rule that each admitted protocol has exactly one admissible configuration fixed at composition, with no capability-driven fallback path.
  * **Price the control plane against R-12-043's own division.** R-12-043 puts protocol state machines in Lustre control planes, and R-05-054 and R-11-016 say what a Lustre node is, a loop-free, statically-sized reaction. What follows is priced rather than free: R-12-043b owes one machine-checked refinement theorem per named control plane, R-12-043c makes each reference state machine a crown-jewel spec enumerated individually in the inventory, and R-12-043e requires the model be curated from a published machine-checked symbolic analysis where one exists, which raises the price of a well-analysed protocol rather than lowering it.

R-17-050's residual, carrier and certification-body acceptance of an open cellular UE stack, is commercial and answers none of it.

  * **Class I because the authority realized is this register's.** The deliverables are register entries and prose in this repository's own documents; the two outside things the item reads, one staging display protocol and one announced mobile feature, are read in order to be declined and credited rather than authored against, so nothing here waits on an external authority to be satisfied.
  * **Say at the display target what the corpus already says generally.** The refusal of a foreign wire is normative and general already: R-12-010 puts all server protocols in one fork-and-frozen typed IDL profile, R-12-013 makes that profile's wire-format mapping a crown-jewel specification, and R-02-001 and R-14-012 refuse every personality and every foreign binary; the same decline is written out by name for 9P, for the Secret Service, for a TPM's command protocol and for the Vulkan and wgpu personalities. What is missing is only the application: [the COSMIC Desktop target](userspace-porting.md#cosmic-desktop-the-shell-and-the-reference-compositor) keeps the Wayland surface model as vocabulary and replaces the global registry, and never says the protocol itself is not spoken. That sentence belongs at its disposition, on the two grounds that decide. **The layer**: a Wayland connection is a control-plane channel, where here a server interface is typed IDL over synchronous endpoints with all capability transfer explicit (R-07-029, R-12-010). **The delegation**: the wire's descriptor passing has no expression at all, descriptors naming only indices into a per-session table with no paths and no ambient references and new authority arriving over the control plane alone (R-12-006), and ring pages carrying no capability-store permission, so authority physically cannot cross the data plane (R-12-007). The ambient registry is refused by that same R-12-006 clause, R-12-075 carrying the observation half it already carries at that entry and not the enumeration.
  * **Two grounds the item refuses at entry, so neither is re-proposed as a repair.** The reference library's implementation language decides nothing here: the adopted start-from speaks the same wire in Rust, `cosmic-comp` riding smithay, so a sentence resting on that language would be contradicted by the closure this document adopts, and where the observation does bear is the roster consequence below. And the disqualifier is not stated as rings against sockets: *ring* is the register's word for the privilege sense R-04-003 and R-07-018 use, an SPSC ring is the bulk data plane beneath a control-plane channel rather than what refuses one, and a socket-shaped absence is not this corpus's to claim while its own differential rig and gdbserver are socket-based host tools. Scope the absence to the target, as the network entry already scopes it for the NIC.
  * **Draw the roster consequence in the porting preamble, where the selection economy is already stated**, rather than inside one target's section. Two premises sit there already, that a foreign binary does not run, ever, and that the selection is uniformly Rust; the sentence they are never joined into is that anything speaking the raw protocol, GTK, Qt and the engines under Firefox and Chromium, reaches this platform only as a source-level re-target against the capability libc, which does not speak that wire either, so a faithful wire server would have no admissible client to serve.
  * **Decline the security-context protocol in [the alternatives register](architectural-alternatives.md)**, beside the display cluster its neighbours occupy and in that document's own shape: the ground, then the disposition, then the honest cost. State the mechanism as it is, or the entry asserts a restriction the protocol does not define: it attaches sandbox-engine, application and instance metadata to a connection created over a listening socket the client supplies and closed on a hangup signal, and what withholds a privileged global from that connection is per-compositor accept-list policy. That is what makes it declinable on this design's own terms rather than on a quality judgment: authority stays enumerable from an ambient registry and is withheld at a server's discretion rather than not held, which is what R-12-006 and R-12-075 delete between them, and its two primitives have no expression here. Write "file descriptor" out in full, the token `FD-n` belonging to [the freeze contract](freeze-measurement-contract.md).
  * **Record one determination and open no work for it.** Presentation-time feedback is answered already, and in the opposite direction: R-15-236c is a landed MUST NOT naming one surface's present callback as exactly the channel such a report would carry, and its commentary in the alternatives register states the objection as observation rather than as schedulability. The premise that would reopen it is false as well, the line-period deadline belonging to the display-scanout reservation (R-11-008, R-15-230) and not to the compositor's slot, whose occupant is permuted at a major-frame boundary (R-11-023) and whose derived bound is taken against the display reservation's next scanout (R-11-022a). The determination lands as one line under [considered and rejected](#considered-and-rejected) and costs nothing else.

So the ruling is which of two acts this is: the clipboard is an ordinary object a grant designates under R-08-036, adding no enumeration, or the composition-time type set is authored and the clipboard is one member of it. Do not conflate that set with R-07-027a's closed three kernel object classes; they are different sets, and R-07-027a's criterion exists because two counts of two different sets were once compared.
  * **Fix the direction asymmetry and book what the consent act costs.** A copy places the caller's own data and mints nothing; a cross-compartment read is the mint, and it is that read R-08-036's consent act governs. The measurement this item owes is therefore how a paste is designated without spending a full prompt, and an entry mediating every paste through the trusted-path agent without answering it has specified a clipboard nobody can use.

    * **The clipboard half closes on the ruling matching the entries.** Under the ordinary-object arm §8.6's enumerations are the same enumerations after the act as before it and every landed entry reads as an instance of R-08-036; under the composition-time-type arm exactly one enumeration is added, some entry owns its membership and some criterion decides its closure, as R-07-027a's does for the kernel's three. It fails on an entry naming a class no entry enumerates, and on any sentence putting the clipboard's mediation behind a permission decision.
* [ ] **Q18 · Bind proof headers to requirement references without copying normative text** · 12 h, range 8–16 · 0.8% · I
  * **Implementation state.** `proofs headers` generates compact requirement references and a fingerprint of their normative text. K-109 checks and repairs the manifest after arithmetic, and K-108 guards its exclusion from authored citations. The register stays authoritative; `proofs headers --show proofs/PartitionContext.v` presents its selected entries as Markdown when needed. Authored modeling rationale remains in the proof. This item's landing note and measured actual are not yet recorded.
  * **Class I because the authority realized is this repository's.** The writer, its checker and the instruction documents are owned here; no outside authority is required.
  * **One owner, one reference manifest.** Each non-generated proof names its register entries in a delimited region and fingerprints their normative bodies. It carries no generated copy of the obligation text. A changed owner invalidates the manifest; repairing that fingerprint records current bytes and does not certify the artifact's interpretation of the requirement. [RingContract.v](../proofs/RingContract.v) remains exclusively owned by `run.py ring emit` and K-89.
  * **Done when** every manifest reaches its `--fix` fixpoint, the exclusion and equality mutants are killed, and the exact authored citation sets and Gallina statements are unchanged before and after generation. The source digests of dependent generated artifacts must agree after the same repair pass.

* [ ] **Q19 · Qualify existing proof tooling and one reusable component**
  * **The current spec owns acceptance, not the language proposal.** R-05-017, R-05-018a and R-05-018b govern these experiments; [the strategy's tooling map](verification-language-strategy.md#existing-tooling-and-implementation-owners) records candidate capabilities and limits. The estimates below are bounded attended agent-session judgments, not conversions of that document's person-week research budgets and not measured savings. Each child can reject a candidate with a reproducible failing comparison; that decision does not claim the rejected tool's functionality or complete another child's gate.

Choose the part relevant to the bounded client; this is not a requirement to install or reproduce every survey entry. Verified Dafny/CakeML and the Bedrock2/Kami example are evidence-endpoint comparators. Rocqet, Pyrosome, Heapster, Crane and the EVM compiler work remain research references without implementation cells.

A compatibility probe uses a disposable or separate switch and never silently downgrades the proof switch. Recurring commands enter through `tools/run.py`, with guest dependencies in WSL; existing host-only gates remain independent of them. No target runtime, compiler, new semantic anchor or language server is built by this item.

**What an item here can claim is a direction, never a measured delta or an additive credit.**

| Change | Performance | Capacity | Implementation effort | Security |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Q1, the claims corrected and the baseline instrumented | Estimates may fall; the hardware is unchanged | Unchanged | Small setup cost, less misdirected work | Clearer claims, no new protection |
| Q2, reusable generated lowering | Uncertain; target optimization still needed | Possibly less runtime or image overhead | The strongest candidate for recurring savings | Fewer manual correspondence gaps where the whole route is checked |
| Q3, the proof and oracle instruments connected | Usually unchanged | Unchanged | More immediate work, less regression uncertainty | Stronger evidence, not universal fault immunity |
| Q4, quantization and smaller inference demand | Can help bandwidth-bound work; quality and compute trade off | More useful payload per byte, not more RAM cells | Moderate integration and validation | No automatic hardening |
| Q5, a better static assignment | Fewer waits, or more useful work fitted | Better utilization only | Solver integration plus the independent check | Isolation preserved while the same constraints hold |
| Q6, explicit external public bulk | May slow generation while improving fit | Potentially a much larger data store | A new interface, its checking and its hardware integration | A new integrity, traffic and availability boundary |
| Q7, authenticated-encrypted sensitive bulk | Usually a direct cost | Metadata consumes space; a larger medium may become acceptable | A substantial new subsystem | Better-defined physical confidentiality and integrity |
| Q8, targeted acceleration | Potentially large locally, limited in total | Area competes with RAM | More hardware integration and proof work | Neutral only after the new obligations are met |
| Q9, bounded compatibility and local workflows | Primarily usability | A resource cost, not a capacity gain | Less porting, at the cost of new interfaces | Each added trust boundary analysed |
| Q10, a smaller product scope | A better chance of meeting latency and power | Less capacity required | A large reduction in the work a useful product needs | A smaller attack and proof surface, fewer features |
| Q11, the reach of run-ahead fetch bounded | Unchanged; a bound on what is fetched is not a bound on what is executed | Unchanged | A register act and a netlist question added to an audit that already runs | A class the model cannot express, closed at the artifact that can |
| Q12, a narrower weight format and a stated prefill term | Possibly a higher admitted rate on the binding term; prefill is a separate quantity | More useful payload per byte, not more cells | A measurement and a register act, no new mechanism | Unchanged |
| Q13, the bring-up interpreter ported | Uncertain until measured here; an upstream figure may invert | Possibly a smaller image | A port plus the admission surface threading would book | Unchanged, unless threading puts code pointers in data |
| Q14, the matrix track reached | Unchanged; this buys oracles, not throughput | Unchanged | A reading and an entry, recurring at each ratification act | Stronger evidence for a surface that has none |
| Q15, a contactless surface decided | Unchanged | Unchanged | A band, a coding, a sequencer event and a compartment, or a booking | A new attacker-facing grammar, or a stated refusal |
| Q16, a messaging scope decided | Unchanged | Unchanged | A compartment and a protocol control plane, or a booking | A new trust anchor if the transport is taken |
| Q17, the display decline named and the clipboard ruled | Unchanged | Unchanged | Two entries and a roster consequence stated | A cross-compartment channel given a consent act rather than left unstated |
| Q18, the cited-entry headers derived | Unchanged | Unchanged | A repair writer and one rule, against a hand-maintained copy in every proof artifact | Unchanged; the exclusion it rests on is what keeps the blast-radius answer honest |
| Q19, proof tooling and reuse qualified | No target speed claim | No target capacity claim | Bounded setup and comparison before any recurring savings are credited | Earlier rejection and audited reuse; no new admitted theorem from tooling alone |
| Q20, one workflow joined to the optimized artifact | Measured against equivalent target baselines; no speed promised | Explicit resource accounting; no physical capacity claim | Setup and held-out maintenance costs measured before expansion | Exact source and artifact evidence required; no guarantee inherited from an earlier executable |

For an optimization that reaches only part of the runtime, the whole-program bound is `1 / ((1 - f) + f / s)` for the fraction `f` of the original time accelerated by `s`, every other cost unchanged; added transfers or a changed schedule invalidate that simplification and are measured separately. External bulk capacity is not a token-rate gain, and compressed data capacity is not a wider physical address space.

**Candidate dependencies are consulted, and none is installed or vendored by this section.** Existing pinned components keep their licence authority in [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md); a new candidate needs a selected immutable revision, a dependency and notice review, and a reproducible invocation before the milestone that would incorporate it reads its terms, and a root licence covers neither bundled dependencies, PDKs, model weights nor every generated artifact. The host tools live apart from the Rocq and Sail switches, platform and interpreter compatibility is checked at the chosen revision rather than inferred from an upstream's Python API, a tool whose supported environment is Linux runs through the WSL dispatch, and an experiment that becomes recurring work enters through [tools/run.py](../tools/run.py).

| Candidate | Recommendation | Benefit and integration boundary | Licence source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Rupicola and Bedrock2 | Q2's first experiment, in the switch M1.6 created | Less repeated manual functional-to-imperative refinement for suitable parsers, once the capability and proof gaps are closed and before a device route is claimed | The MIT and BSD-3-Clause readings [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries |
| The pinned Sail helper properties, QuickChick, `oracle` and `seed` | Q3 connects them before any further verification framework arrives | Coverage of the narrowed semantics and the generated code; a host solver result is not a Rocq proof object | The existing inventory and [tools/README.md](../tools/README.md) |
| Proof interaction, libraries and automation | Q19 qualifies the candidates in [the strategy's tooling map](verification-language-strategy.md#existing-tooling-and-implementation-owners); Q2b and Q3b own their machine connection | Rocq LSP/Pytanque and Alectryon for feedback, selective library and generator reuse, and logic-specific automation only after its primitive rules exist | Each selected upstream's own licence at incorporation, recorded under [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md); the survey admits no dependency |
| Integrated authoring, representation and optimization candidates | Q20 uses the [strategy's focused research comparisons](verification-language-strategy.md#research-that-changes-the-experiments); Q19 and M1.3 retain their qualification and implementation ownership | A selected mechanism must improve the bounded client or close its actual seam; Chamois pass reuse and representation/FFI generation require compatible semantics and proofs rather than a new runtime or assumed CHERI support | Exact upstream and dependency licences before incorporation, including inherited compiler terms and generated artifacts; no dependency is incorporated by the trial's scheduling |
| OR-Tools CP-SAT | Q5 adds it only where enumeration is insufficient | Host-side constrained search, the exact independent admission check keeping the heuristics outside the trust base | [Apache-2.0 root licence](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/or-tools/stable/LICENSE) |
| llama.cpp and `llama-bench` | Q4's host-only workload instrument | Comparison of quality-acceptable model configurations and of prompt and generation behaviour; no certified target port implied | [MIT root licence](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/master/LICENSE) |
| Ramulator2 | Q6, conditional on evaluating conventional external DRAM | Avoids authoring a general DRAM timing simulator; the traffic and scheduling assumptions are adapted and power analysis is added separately | [MIT root licence](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CMU-SAFARI/ramulator2/main/LICENSE) |
| OpenRAM | Q6, conditional on a suitable PDK and a macro experiment | SRAM layout, timing and power views; not a source of qualified gain cells or of a ready high-density monolithic stack | [BSD-3-Clause root licence](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/VLSIDA/OpenRAM/stable/LICENSE) |

**Licensing is an implementation constraint as well.** The CompCert route through SECOMP carries non-commercial terms outside its dual-licensed subset, which [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) decomposes, so a permissive generator does not make a commercially usable compiler chain, and what it recovers of third-party rebuildability is exactly the components it lowers, M1.6 having recorded that the recovery reaches the allocation-light components and never the kernel. The intended use and distribution terms are resolved before a product depends on that route, and the permissive Bedrock2 route is compared without being treated as an immediate replacement, its capability target being absent.

Five shortcuts are worth taking and the section rests on them: generate implementations and proof obligations from one source where that is cheaper than hand-maintaining both, and reuse the domain-specific routes before inventing a universal one; let powerful untrusted search propose a small artifact and a simpler independently justified check admit it, so the search algorithm is never a trusted runtime; reuse host libraries as behavioural comparators and measurement tools without promising they ship; change one architectural variable per comparison and keep current-design and relaxed-design results apart; and reject an option after a small decisive experiment rather than building its whole toolchain to learn that it misses a bandwidth or proof-cost budget. The shortcuts refused are recorded under [considered and rejected](#considered-and-rejected).

  * [ ] **M0.8c · M-class matrix extension and its scratchpad** · 5 h, range 3–7 · 0.3% · X · Parallel with M0.8b · after the M8a gate
    * **Re-priced and deferred past M8a on this item's own finding.** If IME ratifies, the surface R-15-014a (ix) admits is a version pin rather than an authored encoding, so the cell prices a pin and a review rather than an encoding act; and the item gates no milestone here, its scratchpad half being landed and its instruction half conditioned on a margin no backend and no composed image can supply.
    * **The scratchpad half is in the tree and the item stays open on the other half, which the profile has not yet enumerated.** The software-managed scratchpad is an addressable region class ([sys/pma.sail](../model/model/sys/pma.sail)): capability-governed plain memory at a fixed address range, on neither of main memory's two latency classes, coherence-exempt, and not executable, with a composition declaring a wrong one refused rather than carried ([postlude/validate_config.sail](../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail)). The partition switch's single unconditional pass covers exactly the declared range and takes the validity tags with it (R-07-014c, R-15-007r, [extensions/platform/vmclear.sail](../model/model/extensions/platform/vmclear.sail)); the attested devicetree carries the link from a class to its range rather than the range twice (R-09-007, [postlude/device_tree.sail](../model/model/postlude/device_tree.sail)); and R-15-009's standing re-pin obligation is recorded beside the geometry the model declares for the unit ([core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail)). **No instruction is added, and what stops it is a gap in the enumeration rather than a shortfall in the work**: the frozen profile books the matrix extension as a *unit* and names no mnemonic, operand form, or encoding for it, and nothing enters the profile by inheritance (R-15-007j).
    * **The custom-opcode ledger does not move.** M0.6g's three rows spend one opcode between them, M0.6h's four still do, and this item adds no fifth: custom-0 (`0b0001011`) carries `cclear` at `funct3` 000, `vmclear` at 001 and the indexed pair's width-coded 011 and 111, leaving 010, 100, 101 and 110 unspent in that opcode and custom-1 and custom-3 whole for the rows the freeze's single measured act admits (R-15-014a, R-15-067e). The matrix extension is not one of those rows *yet*, and which act admits it is now written down: R-15-014a's second-act delta carries it at **(ix)**, its instruction surface, operand sourcing and encoding together, conditioned on R-15-116's sustained dense-GEMM margin, which no backend and no composed image can supply before M1. So custom-1 and custom-3 stay whole against it exactly as M0.6g and M0.6h left them whole for the rows the freeze's single measured act admits, and spending either here would be modelling a measurement-conditioned provisional row, which is the one thing M0.6g's exit condition forbids.
    * **What remains, and why it is a gap rather than a remainder.** R-15-118's coprocessor line and R-08-003's per-element checks need an instruction to be stated over, so they arrive with the surface and not before it; R-15-116's admission is already taken, dense int8/bf16 inference clearing the 8–10× bar and the extension kept, so what is unwritten is the encoding and not the decision. Closing it needs the profile to enumerate the extension's instructions the way it enumerates every other bespoke row's, which is a register and profile act ahead of a model one (R-15-001a).
    * **The margin the surface is conditioned on now has a normative denominator, and that is the one thing about this item that could still close it the other way.** R-15-116a fixes the RVV expression the margin is taken against as the strongest one the profile admits on the M class, dot products included wherever the standing adoption R-15-067l records has ratified by then, and requires the report to record the denominator's own extension set beside its figure. A batched dot product collects the reduction tree an int8 GEMM otherwise pays per output element and produces eight outputs at once, so it is a term in that denominator rather than in the numerator, and *the admission above was taken against a denominator this profile has since made stronger*. Both branches are live and this item closes on either. If the margin clears, the disposition is as written and the surface is a pin or an authored encoding. If it falls below the threshold, R-15-116 **deletes the extension by its own criterion**, and this item closes by striking its instruction half rather than by authoring one: `vmclear`'s empty matrix inventory (below) becomes empty by construction, R-15-117's software duty and R-15-009's re-pin obligation go with the unit, and custom-1 and custom-3 stay whole for something else. Under §1's value function that is the cheaper outcome, which is why the measurement is worth taking before the second act rather than after it.
    * **The re-pin target R-15-009 names by lineage has an edition, which changes what closing that gap costs.** The **Integrated Matrix Extension** is the IME half of that lineage, and its shape is the one this item's findings derive: no new architected state for matrix data, matrix data held in the existing vector registers, 32-bit encodings, and a stated expectation of low opcode pressure. Its task group's ratification plan puts the specification frozen at v0.9 on 2026-06-27, TSC approval at v0.99 on 2026-08-17, and ratification at v1.0 on 2026-08-27. **AME**, the arm that does introduce architectural state of its own, took a one-year task-group extension in April 2026, and it is the arm this design's deletions argue against.
      **The track is named and re-read at its own tags, and it has not ratified.** The extension's own name is **`Zvvm`**, which the lineage reading could not supply, and the repository's newest tag is **v0.9.0 of 2026-08-24, an ARC review candidate**, above an `integrated-matrix/v0.9-freeze-20260804` freeze tag: no v1.0 tag and no ratified tag exists. Its own plan's ratification date of 2026-08-27 is therefore already missed at the read, by the same margin and in the same way the dot products and the bf16 pair missed theirs, which is the third time this profile has read a fast-track plan against its own tags and found the plan ahead of the artifact.
      So **the disposition is unchanged and its arms are now ordered by a fact rather than a hope**: if `Zvvm` ratifies before R-15-014a's second act, the surface is a version pin plus a review and R-15-009's re-pin obligation acquires a target it can name; if it does not, the fallback is the authored encoding at (ix), spending custom-1 or custom-3 and opening a re-pin obligation of its own. Neither arm is reached before R-15-116's margin is measured, and that measurement can still delete the extension outright, which is the cheaper outcome under §1's value function and is why it is worth taking before either arm is priced. What the read does change is the **standing question a re-pin must answer first**, now askable in the concrete: `Zvvm` holds matrix data in the vector registers, so its geometry is a function of the M class's declared VLEN, and whether [core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail)'s separate matrix geometry survives that is the first thing the pin decides rather than the last.
      Three caveats ride that. The dates are targets rather than history, so the edition is read at the act that would pin it. An *integrated* extension makes the matrix geometry a function of the vector geometry, so IME over the M class's declared VLEN is a question against [core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail) and the first thing a re-pin must answer. And a ratified extension is not thereby an admitted one (R-15-007j), so the second act still admits the surface against R-15-116's margin and still enumerates what it admits.
      What this changes is the disposition rather than the schedule: if IME ratifies, the surface R-15-014a (ix) admits is a version pin rather than an authored encoding, and R-15-009's re-pin obligation acquires a target it can name instead of a lineage it can only watch.
      **The lineage is three tracks and this item's reading covered two.** Beside the attached AME and the integrated IME there is the vector-coupled **VME** (`Zvopmm`), whose matrix multiply takes two vector registers, computes their outer product and accumulates into a matrix accumulator register file, needing no two-dimensional matrix load and store surface and no major CSR bank of its own. R-15-009 now names the set rather than one track, and the third is the one this profile would pay least for, adding nothing against §5's closed CSR table and no load and store forms beside R-15-007e's, at the price of being the least free of the M class's declared VLEN. Which track a re-pin takes is decided at the re-pin, so the read this item owes at that act is over three plans and not two.
    * Net change: 576 lines added and 22 removed across ten files, one of them new.
    * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **65 properties against 62**, three of them new; `verifiedos.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at **270 keys, unchanged**, the region class being a property of a list member rather than a key of the configuration tree; the differential corpus green at 21 of 21 with every digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green and the tree unmoved at 4,466 bytes, no configuration here declaring a scratchpad for it to carry; each of the **eight** new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration and the unmutated profile accepted; the checker green on all 64 rules with every mutant killed; and both type checkers clean.
    * Ten findings.
      * **The profile books the matrix extension as a unit and names no instruction, so there is nothing here to admit.** Every other bespoke row in [isa-profile.md](isa-profile.md) §3 names its instruction, operand form and encoding decision; the matrix row names a unit, a disposition and a re-pin target, and a construct the enumerations do not name is absent (R-15-007j). Three neighbouring decisions are taken and read as though they were this one: no architectural tile file, no block-scale register (R-15-117 keeping de-quantization in software), and outer-product sourcing recorded as an exploration question rather than an ISA commitment. All three are about what the extension does not **hold**, and a surface whose operands come from vector registers and memory needs none of it. What is left undecided is the mnemonic, the operand encoding and the opcode.
      * **The empty profile cell was the symptom and the missing sorting rule was the defect.** No Sail file carries a matrix instruction; what [core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail) holds is geometry, a composition declaring how large a systolic array is rather than a machine knowing what it executes. What sorts the surface is R-15-116's admission against a **sustained dense-GEMM margin**, a measurement against generated output, so the surface belongs to the second act at (ix) rather than to an amendment. R-15-014b now states the missing rule: a unit booked without an instruction surface names the act that will book it, and one booked with neither is a review-gate finding. **So this half is out of scope rather than blocked**, and modelling the surface now would be the measurement-conditioned provisional row M0.6g's exit condition refuses.
      * **A scratchpad is a placement rather than a structure, which decides how much of one a Sail model can carry.** R-15-167 makes it plain memory at a fixed range with no fill, no eviction, no valid or tag array, and no lookup that is not the ordinary address decode, so it is declared beside every other property the composition fixes about a range. That leaves one irreducible new fact, *whose* it is, everything else being a refusal over a field the PMA already had: not coherent, not executable, not on a latency class. R-15-165's own test makes the thinness correct rather than lossy, the contents being a function of an explicit software placement and never of access history.
      * **It is on neither latency class, and stating that relaxes an M0.14 invariant rather than adding a third class.** R-15-247's two classes are two media of *main* memory and R-15-168 makes the scratchpad the local tier above them, so the validator's *every array is on exactly one class* becomes *every array that is not a scratchpad*. A third `MemoryClass` arm would misstate R-15-247's own count, move the figures K-60 holds, and give `memory_class_at` a value no latency constant stands behind.
      * **The matrix half of `vmclear`'s inventory is empty, an enumeration result rather than a milestone's residue.** M0.6h booked two absent members; one lands and the other turns out to be nothing to clear, since R-15-117 keeps de-quantization in software, the tile file is not imported, and R-15-118's coprocessor line leaves the unit no state of its own. What a partition can dirty is the scratchpad and nothing else.
      * **The scratchpad has no size, and no configuration in this tree declares one.** R-15-108 selects scratchpad sizes and the exploration has not run, which is what `frozen` is false for in every class row; a range written into a shipped configuration ahead of it is how a placeholder becomes an architectural input. So the model carries the shape, refuses a wrong one, and carries no range.
      * **The scratchpad is unreachable from the C-class golden emulator, so this item adds no corpus member and its harness installs its own address map.** The frozen emulator is the C class (M0.10) and R-15-165 puts the scratchpads on the V and M datapaths, so no corpus program can address one. The three new `$[test]` properties install a region above every region the configurations declare and restore what they found: a property about a structure no shipped configuration has is a property that must supply it.
      * **A new per-region property does not move the key-set gate, and schema conformance catches it instead.** `memory.regions` is a *list*, so a member's properties are not keys of the configuration tree and both declarations agree at 270 before and after; what bites is the schema, which grew the property and refuses a region without it in either file. An unmoved key count is therefore not evidence that nothing was added to a configuration.
      * **The build tree is one tree, and a second worktree's build fails at `configure` rather than at compile.** `cmake` records the source directory in its cache, so a lane building the canonical build directory from a different checkout is refused with a cache mismatch before a line is compiled. The lever needed no tool change, [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) already reading `VOS_BUILD_DIR` and `VOS_LOG_DIR`, and it is the mechanism by which two items that both write `model/` serialize.
      * **This item owes the checker no rule, because the fact it would hold is a model fact rather than a document one.** The one cross-artifact agreement it creates is between a class's declared `scratchpad_bytes` and the address map's declared range, both in the configuration the Sail validator reads, so the refusal fires on every run of every loop rather than on a document sweep. A checker rule over the same pair would be a second reader of one file.
